=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a tree with N vertices. The i-th edge (1 \leq i \leq N-1) connects vertices u_i and v_i bidirectionally.
Adding one undirected edge to the given tree always yields a graph with exactly one cycle.
Among such graphs, how many satisfy all of the following conditions?

- The graph is simple.
- All vertices in the cycle have degree 3.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
u_1 v_1
u_2 v_2
\vdots
u_{N-1} v_{N-1}

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 3 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq u_i, v_i \leq N
- The given graph is a tree.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

6
1 2
2 3
3 4
4 5
3 6

Sample Output 1

1

Adding an edge connecting vertices 2 and 4 yields a simple graph where all vertices in the cycle have degree 3, so it satisfies the conditions.

Sample Input 2

7
1 2
2 7
3 5
7 3
6 2
4 7

Sample Output 2

0

There are cases where no graphs satisfy the conditions.

Sample Input 3

15
1 15
11 14
2 10
1 7
9 8
6 9
4 12
14 5
4 9
8 11
7 4
1 13
3 6
11 10

Sample Output 3

6

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

- Then create dictionary comp_count[root] = number of distinct deg2 vertices adjacent to component.

   seen = set of roots of deg3 neighbors
   For each neighbor y:

Because deg2 vertex has at most 2 neighbors, seen small. If both neighbors deg3 and same root (shouldn't happen), avoid double count. If both deg3 different roots, count in both.
- Answer = sum c*(c-1)//2 for c in comp_count.values().

Let's verify with sample 1 using code mentally.

Implementation details:
```
import sys
def main():
    input=sys.stdin.readline
    N=int(input())
    adj=[[] for _ in range(N+1)]
    edges=[]
    for _ in range(N-1):
        u,v=map(int,input().split())
        adj[u].append(v); adj[v].append(u)
        edges.append((u,v))
    deg=[len(adj[i]) for i in range(N+1)]
    parent=list(range(N+1))
    size=[1]*(N+1)
    def find(x):
        while parent[x]!=x:
            parent[x]=parent[parent[x]]
            x=parent[x]
        return x
    def union(a,b):
        ra=find(a); rb=find(b)
        if ra==rb: return
        if size[ra]<size[rb]: ra,rb=rb,ra
        parent[rb]=ra; size[ra]+=size[rb]
    for u,v in edges:
        if deg[u]==3 and deg[v]==3:
            union(u,v)
    comp_count={}
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        if deg[x]==2:
            seen=set()
            for y in adj[x]:
                if deg[y]==3:
                    r=find(y)
                    if r not in seen:
                        seen.add(r)
                        comp_count[r]=comp_count.get(r,0)+1
    ans=0
    for c in comp_count.values():
        ans += c*(c-1)//2
    print(ans)
if __name__=="__main__": main()
```
Need ensure parent for deg3 vertices after union. If a deg3 vertex has no union, parent itself. If deg2 neighbor y deg3, find(y) okay. If deg[y]==3. Good.

Could optimize by not storing edges? Need union after deg computed; can compute deg while reading and store edges. Fine.

Potential issue with recursion? none.

Let's draft final code:
```
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    it = iter(data)
    N = next(it)
    adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]
    edges = []
    for _ in range(N-1):
        u = next(it); v = next(it)
        adj[u].append(v); adj[v].append(u)
        edges.append((u,v))
    deg = [0]*(N+1)
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        deg[i] = len(adj[i])
    parent = list(range(N+1))
    size = [1]*(N+1)
    def find(x):
        while parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
            x = parent[x]
        return x
    def union(a,b):
        ra = find(a); rb = find(b)
        if ra == rb:
            return
        if size[ra] < size[rb]:
            ra, rb = rb, ra
        parent[rb] = ra
        size[ra] += size[rb]
    for u,v in edges:
        if deg[u] == 3 and deg[v] == 3:
            union(u,v)
    comp_count = {}
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        if deg[x] == 2:
            seen = set()
            for y in adj[x]:
                if deg[y] == 3:
                    r = find(y)
                    if r not in seen:
                        seen.add(r)
                        comp_count[r] = comp_count.get(r, 0) + 1
    ans = 0
    for c in comp_count.values():
        ans += c * (c - 1) // 2
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
Need check if using data list of ints memory: 2*(N-1)+1 ~ 4e5 ints, okay. Actually N=2e5, edges 199999, ints ~400k, memory ~11 MB plus adj, okay. Could use readline to reduce memory but fine. N=int(input())
adj=[[] for _ in range(N+1)]

for _ in range(N-1):
    u,v=map(int,input().split())
    adj[u].append(v); adj[v].append(u)

Let's think if there is any hidden nuance: "Adding one undirected edge to the given tree always yields a graph with exactly one cycle." Actually if you add an edge between any two vertices, including adjacent, in a simple graph, adding an edge that already exists is not allowed as an "undirected edge" because the graph would not be simple? They say "Adding one undirected edge to the given tree always yields a graph with exactly one cycle." In graph theory, adding an edge between two non-adjacent vertices creates exactly one cycle. If between adjacent, it creates a multigraph with two parallel edges and a cycle of length 2, but not a simple graph. They then require graph is simple, so invalid. Good.

Now, let's think about possible optimization of DSU find with iterative path compression:
```
def find(x):
    while parent[x] != x:
        parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
        x = parent[x]
    return x
```
This is path halving. Works. Union by size.

Important! We defined D3 components as connected components of subgraph induced by all degree3 vertices. Suppose a valid path has internal vertices all deg3. Those internal vertices form a path in the D3-induced subgraph. But a deg3 internal vertex may have off-cycle deg3 neighbors. That off-cycle deg3 neighbor is also in the same D3 component, but not on the cycle. Our counting of D3 components includes all deg3 vertices connected via deg3 edges, including off-cycle branches. Does this cause overcount? Let's think.

Consider a deg3 component C that includes a path of deg3 vertices plus extra deg3 branches. We count degree2 vertices adjacent to any vertex in C. If two degree2 vertices u,v are adjacent to C, the path between them through C is unique and may pass through some deg3 vertices, but not necessarily all of C. It will not include off-cycle deg3 branches. That's fine. The path from u to v uses the unique path in C between their attachment points. Since C is a tree (subgraph of tree), all vertices on that path are deg3. So valid. Extra branches don't matter. So counting by entire component is okay.

But wait, if a deg3 internal vertex on the cycle has an off-cycle deg3 neighbor, then that off-cycle deg3 neighbor is in the same D3 component. - u to v: u-b-a-c-v internal b,a,c deg3 valid.
- u to w: u-b-a-d-w internal b,a,d deg3 valid.
- v to w: v-c-a-d-w internal c,a,d deg3 valid.
Yes. The path between any two deg2 vertices attached to the same tree component C is the unique path in C between attachment points, all deg3. So valid. Extra branches are just part of the tree component; the unique path between the two attachment points won't include branches not between them. Good.
